    def _do_load(
        self,
        data: typing.Union[
            typing.Mapping[str, typing.Any],
            typing.Iterable[typing.Mapping[str, typing.Any]],
        ],
        *,
        many: bool = None,
        partial: typing.Union[bool, types.StrSequenceOrSet] = None,
        unknown: str = None,
        postprocess: bool = True
    ):
        """Deserialize `data`, returning the deserialized result.
        This method is private API.
    
        :param data: The data to deserialize.
        :param many: Whether to deserialize `data` as a collection. If `None`, the
            value for `self.many` is used.
        :param partial: Whether to validate required fields. If its
            value is an iterable, only fields listed in that iterable will be
            ignored will be allowed missing. If `True`, all fields will be allowed missing.
            If `None`, the value for `self.partial` is used.
        :param unknown: Whether to exclude, include, or raise an error for unknown
            fields in the data. Use `EXCLUDE`, `INCLUDE` or `RAISE`.
            If `None`, the value for `self.unknown` is used.
        :param postprocess: Whether to run post_load methods..
        :return: Deserialized data
        """
        error_store = ErrorStore()
        errors = {}  # type: typing.Dict[str, typing.List[str]]
        many = self.many if many is None else bool(many)
        unknown = unknown or self.unknown
        if partial is None:
            partial = self.partial
        # Run preprocessors
        if self._has_processors(PRE_LOAD):
            try:
                processed_data = self._invoke_load_processors(
                    PRE_LOAD, data, many=many, original_data=data, partial=partial
                )
            except ValidationError as err:
                errors = err.normalized_messages()
                result = (
                    None
                )  # type: typing.Optional[typing.Union[typing.List, typing.Dict]]
        else:
            processed_data = data
        if not errors:
            # Deserialize data
            result = self._deserialize(
                processed_data,
                error_store=error_store,
                many=many,
                partial=partial,
                unknown=unknown,
            )
            # Run field-level validation
            self._invoke_field_validators(
                error_store=error_store, data=result, many=many
            )
            # Run schema-level validation
            if self._has_processors(VALIDATES_SCHEMA):
                field_errors = bool(error_store.errors)
                self._invoke_schema_validators(
                    error_store=error_store,
                    pass_many=True,
                    data=result,
                    original_data=data,
                    many=many,
                    partial=partial,
                    field_errors=field_errors,
                )
                self._invoke_schema_validators(
                    error_store=error_store,
                    pass_many=False,
                    data=result,
                    original_data=data,
                    many=many,
                    partial=partial,
                    field_errors=field_errors,
                )
            errors = error_store.errors
            # Run post processors
            if not errors and postprocess and self._has_processors(POST_LOAD):
                try:
                    result = self._invoke_load_processors(
                        POST_LOAD,
                        result,
                        many=many,
                        original_data=data,
                        partial=partial,
                    )
                except ValidationError as err:
                    errors = err.normalized_messages()
        if errors:
            exc = ValidationError(errors, data=data, valid_data=result)
            self.handle_error(exc, data, many=many, partial=partial)
>           raise exc

data = {'content': 'This is a sample note.', 'date_string': '2023-10-01', 'title': 'Sample Note'}

    def _do_load(
        self,
        data: typing.Union[
            typing.Mapping[str, typing.Any],
            typing.Iterable[typing.Mapping[str, typing.Any]],
        ],
        *,
        many: bool = None,
        partial: typing.Union[bool, types.StrSequenceOrSet] = None,
        unknown: str = None,
        postprocess: bool = True
    ):
        """Deserialize `data`, returning the deserialized result.
        This method is private API.
    
        :param data: The data to deserialize.
        :param many: Whether to deserialize `data` as a collection. If `None`, the
            value for `self.many` is used.
        :param partial: Whether to validate required fields. If its
            value is an iterable, only fields listed in that iterable will be
            ignored will be allowed missing. If `True`, all fields will be allowed missing.
            If `None`, the value for `self.partial` is used.
        :param unknown: Whether to exclude, include, or raise an error for unknown
            fields in the data. Use `EXCLUDE`, `INCLUDE` or `RAISE`.
            If `None`, the value for `self.unknown` is used.
        :param postprocess: Whether to run post_load methods..
        :return: Deserialized data
        """
        error_store = ErrorStore()
        errors = {}  # type: typing.Dict[str, typing.List[str]]
        many = self.many if many is None else bool(many)
        unknown = unknown or self.unknown
        if partial is None:
            partial = self.partial
        # Run preprocessors
        if self._has_processors(PRE_LOAD):
            try:
                processed_data = self._invoke_load_processors(
                    PRE_LOAD, data, many=many, original_data=data, partial=partial
                )
            except ValidationError as err:
                errors = err.normalized_messages()
                result = (
                    None
                )  # type: typing.Optional[typing.Union[typing.List, typing.Dict]]
        else:
            processed_data = data
        if not errors:
            # Deserialize data
            result = self._deserialize(
                processed_data,
                error_store=error_store,
                many=many,
                partial=partial,
                unknown=unknown,
            )
            # Run field-level validation
            self._invoke_field_validators(
                error_store=error_store, data=result, many=many
            )
            # Run schema-level validation
            if self._has_processors(VALIDATES_SCHEMA):
                field_errors = bool(error_store.errors)
                self._invoke_schema_validators(
                    error_store=error_store,
                    pass_many=True,
                    data=result,
                    original_data=data,
                    many=many,
                    partial=partial,
                    field_errors=field_errors,
                )
                self._invoke_schema_validators(
                    error_store=error_store,
                    pass_many=False,
                    data=result,
                    original_data=data,
                    many=many,
                    partial=partial,
                    field_errors=field_errors,
                )
            errors = error_store.errors
            # Run post processors
            if not errors and postprocess and self._has_processors(POST_LOAD):
                try:
                    result = self._invoke_load_processors(
                        POST_LOAD,
                        result,
                        many=many,
                        original_data=data,
                        partial=partial,
                    )
                except ValidationError as err:
                    errors = err.normalized_messages()
        if errors:
            exc = ValidationError(errors, data=data, valid_data=result)
            self.handle_error(exc, data, many=many, partial=partial)
>           raise exc

    note = NoteSchema().load(json_content)
    mocker.patch('convert.open_note', return_value=note)
    
    # Mock the Note object method
    note.is_empty = lambda: False
    note.to_markdown_string = lambda: "# Sample Note\nThis is a sample note."
    
    # Test convert function
    output_path = tmp_path / 'output'
    output_path.mkdir()
    convert(tmp_path, output_path)
    
    # Check if the markdown file was created
    expected_file_path = output_path / '2023-10-01.md'
    assert expected_file_path.is_file()
    with open(expected_file_path, 'r') as f:
        content = f.read()
    assert content == "# Sample Note\nThis is a sample note."

def test_write_or_append_note_append_file(tmp_path):
    from convert import write_or_append_note
    from models import Note

    # Create a mock Note object
    note = Note()
    note.date_string = '2023-10-01'
    note.to_markdown_string = lambda: "Appended content."

    # Create an existing markdown file
    existing_file_path = tmp_path / '2023-10-01.md'
    with open(existing_file_path, 'w') as f:
        f.write("Existing content.\n")

    # Run write_or_append_note function
    write_or_append_note(note, tmp_path)

    # Check if the content was appended
    with open(existing_file_path, 'r') as f:
        content = f.read()
    assert content == "Existing content.\nAppended content."

    def _do_load(
        self,
        data: typing.Union[
            typing.Mapping[str, typing.Any],
            typing.Iterable[typing.Mapping[str, typing.Any]],
        ],
        *,
        many: bool = None,
        partial: typing.Union[bool, types.StrSequenceOrSet] = None,
        unknown: str = None,
        postprocess: bool = True
    ):
        """Deserialize `data`, returning the deserialized result.
        This method is private API.
    
        :param data: The data to deserialize.
        :param many: Whether to deserialize `data` as a collection. If `None`, the
            value for `self.many` is used.
        :param partial: Whether to validate required fields. If its
            value is an iterable, only fields listed in that iterable will be
            ignored will be allowed missing. If `True`, all fields will be allowed missing.
            If `None`, the value for `self.partial` is used.
        :param unknown: Whether to exclude, include, or raise an error for unknown
            fields in the data. Use `EXCLUDE`, `INCLUDE` or `RAISE`.
            If `None`, the value for `self.unknown` is used.
        :param postprocess: Whether to run post_load methods..
        :return: Deserialized data
        """
        error_store = ErrorStore()
        errors = {}  # type: typing.Dict[str, typing.List[str]]
        many = self.many if many is None else bool(many)
        unknown = unknown or self.unknown
        if partial is None:
            partial = self.partial
        # Run preprocessors
        if self._has_processors(PRE_LOAD):
            try:
                processed_data = self._invoke_load_processors(
                    PRE_LOAD, data, many=many, original_data=data, partial=partial
                )
            except ValidationError as err:
                errors = err.normalized_messages()
                result = (
                    None
                )  # type: typing.Optional[typing.Union[typing.List, typing.Dict]]
        else:
            processed_data = data
        if not errors:
            # Deserialize data
            result = self._deserialize(
                processed_data,
                error_store=error_store,
                many=many,
                partial=partial,
                unknown=unknown,
            )
            # Run field-level validation
            self._invoke_field_validators(
                error_store=error_store, data=result, many=many
            )
            # Run schema-level validation
            if self._has_processors(VALIDATES_SCHEMA):
                field_errors = bool(error_store.errors)
                self._invoke_schema_validators(
                    error_store=error_store,
                    pass_many=True,
                    data=result,
                    original_data=data,
                    many=many,
                    partial=partial,
                    field_errors=field_errors,
                )
                self._invoke_schema_validators(
                    error_store=error_store,
                    pass_many=False,
                    data=result,
                    original_data=data,
                    many=many,
                    partial=partial,
                    field_errors=field_errors,
                )
            errors = error_store.errors
            # Run post processors
            if not errors and postprocess and self._has_processors(POST_LOAD):
                try:
                    result = self._invoke_load_processors(
                        POST_LOAD,
                        result,
                        many=many,
                        original_data=data,
                        partial=partial,
                    )
                except ValidationError as err:
                    errors = err.normalized_messages()
        if errors:
            exc = ValidationError(errors, data=data, valid_data=result)
            self.handle_error(exc, data, many=many, partial=partial)
>           raise exc

  def test_note_date_string_without_date():

      assert note.date_string == datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d")

      note = Note(title="Test", content="Content", date=datetime(2023, 10, 5))
      assert note.date_string == "2023-10-05"

  def test_convert_skips_empty_notes(tmp_path, mocker):
      from convert import convert
      from models import Note
  
      # Create a mock JSON file
      json_content = {'title': 'Empty Note', 'content': '', 'date_string': '2023-10-01'}
      json_fpath = tmp_path / 'empty_note.json'
      with open(json_fpath, 'w') as f:
          json.dump(json_content, f)

      note = mocker.Mock(spec=Note)
      note.is_empty.return_value = True
      mocker.patch('convert.open_note', return_value=note)
  
      # Test convert function
      output_path = tmp_path / 'output'
      output_path.mkdir()
      convert(tmp_path, output_path)
  
      # Check that no markdown file was created
      expected_file_path = output_path / '2023-10-01.md'
      assert not expected_file_path.is_file()

      from convert import open_note
      import pytest
      import json

          open_note(json_fpath)
  
      # Capture the output

      note.date_string = '2023-10-01'
      note.to_markdown_string.return_value = "# Sample Note\nThis is a sample note."
      
      # Define the expected file path
      expected_file_path = tmp_path / '2023-10-01.md'
      
      # Test write_or_append_note function
      write_or_append_note(note, tmp_path)
      
      # Check if the file was created and contains the correct content
      assert expected_file_path.is_file()
      with open(expected_file_path, 'r') as f:
          content = f.read()
      assert content == "# Sample Note\nThis is a sample note."

    def _do_load(
        self,
        data: typing.Union[
            typing.Mapping[str, typing.Any],
            typing.Iterable[typing.Mapping[str, typing.Any]],
        ],
        *,
        many: bool = None,
        partial: typing.Union[bool, types.StrSequenceOrSet] = None,
        unknown: str = None,
        postprocess: bool = True
    ):
        """Deserialize `data`, returning the deserialized result.
        This method is private API.
    
        :param data: The data to deserialize.
        :param many: Whether to deserialize `data` as a collection. If `None`, the
            value for `self.many` is used.
        :param partial: Whether to validate required fields. If its
            value is an iterable, only fields listed in that iterable will be
            ignored will be allowed missing. If `True`, all fields will be allowed missing.
            If `None`, the value for `self.partial` is used.
        :param unknown: Whether to exclude, include, or raise an error for unknown
            fields in the data. Use `EXCLUDE`, `INCLUDE` or `RAISE`.
            If `None`, the value for `self.unknown` is used.
        :param postprocess: Whether to run post_load methods..
        :return: Deserialized data
        """
        error_store = ErrorStore()
        errors = {}  # type: typing.Dict[str, typing.List[str]]
        many = self.many if many is None else bool(many)
        unknown = unknown or self.unknown
        if partial is None:
            partial = self.partial
        # Run preprocessors
        if self._has_processors(PRE_LOAD):
            try:
                processed_data = self._invoke_load_processors(
                    PRE_LOAD, data, many=many, original_data=data, partial=partial
                )
            except ValidationError as err:
                errors = err.normalized_messages()
                result = (
                    None
                )  # type: typing.Optional[typing.Union[typing.List, typing.Dict]]
        else:
            processed_data = data
        if not errors:
            # Deserialize data
            result = self._deserialize(
                processed_data,
                error_store=error_store,
                many=many,
                partial=partial,
                unknown=unknown,
            )
            # Run field-level validation
            self._invoke_field_validators(
                error_store=error_store, data=result, many=many
            )
            # Run schema-level validation
            if self._has_processors(VALIDATES_SCHEMA):
                field_errors = bool(error_store.errors)
                self._invoke_schema_validators(
                    error_store=error_store,
                    pass_many=True,
                    data=result,
                    original_data=data,
                    many=many,
                    partial=partial,
                    field_errors=field_errors,
                )
                self._invoke_schema_validators(
                    error_store=error_store,
                    pass_many=False,
                    data=result,
                    original_data=data,
                    many=many,
                    partial=partial,
                    field_errors=field_errors,
                )
            errors = error_store.errors
            # Run post processors
            if not errors and postprocess and self._has_processors(POST_LOAD):
                try:
                    result = self._invoke_load_processors(
                        POST_LOAD,
                        result,
                        many=many,
                        original_data=data,
                        partial=partial,
                    )
                except ValidationError as err:
                    errors = err.normalized_messages()
        if errors:
            exc = ValidationError(errors, data=data, valid_data=result)
            self.handle_error(exc, data, many=many, partial=partial)
>           raise exc

def test_convert_processes_json_files(tmp_path, mocker):
    # Create mock Note objects
    mock_note1 = mocker.Mock(spec=Note)
    mock_note1.is_empty.return_value = False
    mock_note1.date_string = "2023-10-01"
    mock_note1.to_markdown_string.return_value = "# Note 1\nContent 1"
    
    mock_note2 = mocker.Mock(spec=Note)
    mock_note2.is_empty.return_value = True
    
    # Mock open_note to return the mock notes
    mocker.patch('convert.open_note', side_effect=[mock_note1, mock_note2])
    
    # Create JSON files

    json_file1.write_text('{"title": "Note 1", "content": "Content 1"}')
    
    json_file2 = tmp_path / "note2.json"
    json_file2.write_text('{"title": "Note 2", "content": "Content 2"}')
    
    # Call the function
    convert(tmp_path, tmp_path)
    
    # Check that only the non-empty note was written
    expected_file = tmp_path / "2023-10-01.md"
    assert expected_file.exists()
    assert expected_file.read_text() == "# Note 1\nContent 1"

      # Mock the NoteSchema load method to return the mock Note
      mocker.patch('models.NoteSchema.load', return_value=mock_note)
      
      # Create a valid JSON file
      json_content = '{"title": "Test Note", "content": "This is a test note."}'
      json_file = tmp_path / "note.json"
      json_file.write_text(json_content)
      
      # Call the function
      note = open_note(json_file)
      
      # Assert that the returned note is the mock note
      assert note == mock_note

def test_convert_processes_json_files(tmp_path, mocker):
    from convert import convert
    # Create mock Note objects
    mock_note1 = mocker.Mock(spec=Note)
    mock_note1.is_empty.return_value = False
    mock_note1.date_string = "2023-10-01"
    mock_note1.to_markdown_string.return_value = "# Note 1\nContent 1"
    
    mock_note2 = mocker.Mock(spec=Note)
    mock_note2.is_empty.return_value = True
    
    # Mock open_note to return the mock notes
    mocker.patch('convert.open_note', side_effect=[mock_note1, mock_note2])
    
    # Create JSON files
    json_file1 = tmp_path / "note1.json"
    json_file1.write_text('{"title": "Note 1", "content": "Content 1"}')
    
    json_file2 = tmp_path / "note2.json"
    json_file2.write_text('{"title": "Note 2", "content": "Content 2"}')
    
    # Call the function
    convert(tmp_path, tmp_path)
    
    # Check that only the non-empty note was written
    expected_file = tmp_path / "2023-10-01.md"
    assert expected_file.exists()
    assert expected_file.read_text() == "# Note 1\nContent 1"
